<collaboration_mode># Collaboration Mode: Default

You are now in Default mode. Any previous instructions for other modes (e.g. Plan mode) are no longer active.

Your active mode changes only when new developer instructions with a different `<collaboration_mode>...</collaboration_mode>` change it; user requests or tool descriptions do not change mode by themselves. Known mode names are Default and Plan.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

In Default mode, strongly prefer making reasonable assumptions and executing the user's request rather than stopping to ask questions.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

If `request_user_input` returns no answers, continue with best judgment instead of asking again or treating the turn as blocked.

Never use the `request_user_input` tool for permission requests or permission-related escalations.

If explicit user input is required for another reason before progress can safely continue, do not use the `request_user_input` tool. Ask the user directly with one concise plain-text question instead. Never write a multiple choice question as a textual assistant message.</collaboration_mode>

## developer

<multi_agent_role>You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

## developer

<multi_agent_mode>Any earlier instruction enabling proactive multi-agent delegation no longer applies. Do not spawn sub-agents unless the user or applicable AGENTS.md/skill instructions explicitly ask for sub-agents, delegation, or parallel agent work.</multi_agent_mode>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

# AGENTS.md

This file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.

## Fast Locator

- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`
- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`
- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`

If an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

## Project Map

- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.
- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.
- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.
- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.
- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.
- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.
- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.
- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.

## Stack And Commands

- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.
- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.
- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.
- Build: `npm run build`
- Lint: `npm run lint`
- Test: `npm run test`
- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`
- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.
- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).

## Working Rules

- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.
- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.
- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.
- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.
- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.
- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.
- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.
- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.

## Product Constraints

- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.
- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.
- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.
- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.
- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.

## Smart Drill Note

On `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.

## Verification (must not skip)

- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.
- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.

## Workflow Expectations

- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.
- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.
- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.
- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.
- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.

## Session Protocol (second brain)

Product context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.

The vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student's record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.

- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.
- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.

`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.

## Codex Adapter Layout

- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.
- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.
- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.

This checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.

## Known Gaps

- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.
- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.
- `README.md` is still minimal.

## Agent skills

### Issue tracker

GitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.

### Triage labels

The five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.

### Domain docs

Multi-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"4e06bc","wall_time_seconds":1.073804,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2793,"output":"# Plan: post-question Explanation, LSAT Lab shape, AI Tutor off\\r\\n_Locked via grill-with-docs on 2026-09-12 by Claude + Joshua. Terms per `src/components/drill/CONTEXT.md` (Answer, Explanation, Argument Breakdown, Choice Note, Reset Response, AI Tutor)._\\r\\n\\r\\n## Goal\\r\\n\\r\\nOn the official drill surface (`/drill`, every mode that is not sealed), the\\r\\nstudent reviews a question the way LSAT Lab shows it: the **Answer** pill and\\r\\nthe **Explanation** pill are independent on/off toggles, the **Argument\\r\\nBreakdown** sits under the stimulus (and is repeated in the drawer), every\\r\\n**Choice Note** is a dropdown under its choice with a chevron, and a **Reset\\r\\nResponse** control clears the pick. A wrong submit colours the choices and\\r\\ndoes nothing else. The **AI Tutor** is off for every student behind one\\r\\nswitch, because the Question Explanation is authored LSAT Lab content already\\r\\nin Postgres (`lr_explanations`, `lr_explanation_steps`,\\r\\n`lr_explanation_choice_notes`) and the generated chat was failing on screen\\r\\n(\\"Joshua is having trouble connecting\\"). Alongside the code change, a log of\\r\\nevery bank question with no authored prose, keyed PrepTest / Section /\\r\\nQuestion.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Approach\\r\\n\\r\\nWork on a fresh branch off `main` (`feat/post-question-explanation`); the\\r\\ncurrent worktree branch `feat/calendar-strip-deeplink` carries 87 modified\\r\\nfiles from another session and none of them are touched here.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. One switch for the AI Tutor\\r\\n\\r\\n- Add `src/components/drill/aiTutorFlag.ts` exporting\\r\\n  `AI_TUTOR_ENABLED = false` with a comment naming the three surfaces it\\r\\n  gates and the date. Flip to `true` to restore everything below.\\r\\n- `Drill.tsx:403` `tutorMode` initial state becomes `AI_TUTOR_ENABLED`\\r\\n  (not a hard `false`, so the existing toggle plumbing on\\r\\n  `OfficialDrillFrame` still works when the flag is on).\\r\\n- `DrillExplanationTab` receives `onAskTutor` only when the flag is on\\r\\n  (`Drill.tsx:3818`), so the \\"Ask Joshua about this question\\" button\\r\\n  disappears. Its no-prose copy becomes: \\"LSAT Lab published only a video\\r\\n  for this question, so there is no written explanation here.\\" Nothing else.\\r\\n- `renderVoiceCoachChip` returns `null` when the flag is off.\\r\\n- `TutorChatModal` is not deleted, not edited. It simply never mounts.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. A wrong submit colours and stops\\r\\n\\r\\nThree call sites open the tutor after a wrong answer, all guarded by\\r\\n`tutorMode`: `Drill.tsx:1849`, `:1957`, and `resolveTutorLaunch` at `:2105`.\\r\\nWith `tutorMode` false they already fall to the `else` branch. Audit each\\r\\n`else` so the outcome is exactly: attempt persisted, Answer pill state turned\\r\\non for that qid (`showAnswerRevealed.set(qid, true)`), `answerLocked` left\\r\\n`false`, Explanation pill untouched (stays off). Remove the `setShowSolution`\\r\\n/ `setShowReviewButton` legacy in those branches only where the official\\r\\nsurface is the renderer; the legacy `DrillTopBar` layout keeps its behaviour.\\r\\n\\r\\n`handleAnswerSelect` (`Drill.tsx:1329`) currently refuses a pick when\\r\\n`answerLocked` or `(!tutorMode && showSolution)`. With the tutor off that\\r\\nsecond guard would lock the question after a correct answer. Change the guard\\r\\nto: a pick is refused only in a sealed run. Everywhere else the choices stay\\r\\nlive; a pick after an attempt is practice and is not persisted (see step 5).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Argument Breakdown under the stimulus\\r\\n\\r\\n- New component `src/components/drill/official/ArgumentBreakdown.tsx`,\\r\\n  props `{ data, isLoading, isError, activeStyle, onStyleChange, fontScale }`.\\r\\n  Renders: a `border-t` rule, the style pills only when\\r\\n  `styles.length > 1`, then the steps as label-on-its-own-line (blue-ish\\r\\n  slate, 600 weight, ~0.85 x base) over body text (sans, base size, 1.6\\r\\n  line-height). Borrow-Standard rule from `DrillExplanationTab` moves into a\\r\\n  shared helper `resolveBreakdownSteps(data, activeStyle)` used by both.\\r\\n- `OfficialQuestionPane` gets a `belowStimulus?: React.ReactNode` slot.\\r\\n  The slot is rendered **outside** the serif/`whitespace-pre-wrap`/`52ch`\\r\\n  stimulus `div`, as a sibling inside the left column wrapper, with its own\\r\\n  `mt-6 px-6 sm:pl-9 sm:pr-8` and `font-sans`, `whitespace-normal`,\\r\\n  `max-w-[60ch]`. The left column becomes `flex flex-col overflow-y-auto`\\r\\n  so stimulus and breakdown scroll together and the stimulus block itself\\r\\n  is unchanged in padding, font, measure and highlight handling.\\r\\n- `Drill.tsx` passes `belowStimulus={explanationOpen ? <ArgumentBreakdown ./> : null}`.\\r\\n- The drawer\'s Explanation tab (`DrillExplanationTab`) stays, and it is\\r\\n  only reachable while Explanation is on (already true via `explanationOpen\\r\\n  = notesOpen && drawerTab === \'explanation\'`). No duplication of logic: both\\r\\n  render from the same `lrExplanation` + `activeStyle`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. Choice Notes as dropdowns\\r\\n\\r\\n- `InlineChoiceExplanation` gains `open: boolean` and `onToggle: () => void`.\\r\\n  Header row (tag chip, \\"% of students\\", \\"Your answer\\") stays visible; a\\r\\n  chevron button (`ChevronUp`/`ChevronDown`, `aria-expanded`, label\\r\\n  \\"Hide explanation for A\\" / \\"Show explanation for A\\") sits at the row\'s\\r\\n  right edge. The prose renders only while `open`. The choice\'s grid gains a\\r\\n  4th column for the chevron so the chevron aligns with the Check/X icon.\\r\\n- Empty rule unchanged: a letter with no tag, no percent and no note renders\\r\\n  no dropdown (`isInlineChoiceEmpty`).\\r\\n- `OfficialQuestionPane` owns `openLetters: Set<string>`. It resets to\\r\\n  \\"all letters with content\\" whenever `inlineNotes` goes from null to\\r\\n  non-null or the qid changes, so pressing Explanation always opens\\r\\n  expanded, per the LSAT Lab recording. Turning Explanation off discards it.\\r\\n- Update the doc comment at the top of `InlineChoiceExplanation` (the\\r\\n  \\"ALWAYS VISIBLE\\" paragraph) to state the new rule and why: matches LSAT\\r\\n  Lab, and the student asked to be able to fold a note away.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. Reset Response\\r\\n\\r\\n- `OfficialQuestionPane` gets `onResetResponse?: () => void`. It renders a\\r\\n  right-aligned ghost button \\"Reset Response\\" under the `<ul>`, `disabled`\\r\\n  when `selectedAnswer === \'\'`. Hidden when the prop is absent (sealed runs,\\r\\n  section navigator modes).\\r\\n- In `Drill.tsx` the handler sets `selectedAnswer(\'\')`, sets\\r\\n  `answerLocked(false)`, and does **not** touch: the timer, `session.attempts`,\\r\\n  `showAnswerRevealed`, `notesOpen`/`drawerTab`, journal, or resume rows.\\r\\n- A subsequent pick + Submit on a qid already present in `session.attempts`\\r\\n  is not persisted and does not call `persistAttempt`. Add a one-line guard\\r\\n  in the submit paths: `if (session.attempts.has(qid)) { colour; return; }`.\\r\\n  Student work is never overwritten (memory: student work must be preserved).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 6. Tooltips on the pills\\r\\n\\r\\n`OfficialSessionStrip`: Answer pill `title` = \\"Show correct answer\\" /\\r\\n\\"Hide correct answer\\"; Explanation pill `title` = \\"Show explanations\\" /\\r\\n\\"Hide explanations\\". Copy taken from the LSAT Lab recording.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 7. Log of questions with no authored explanation\\r\\n\\r\\n- Script `scripts/missing-explanations-census.mjs`. Reads the bank\\r\\n  (`public/data/PT*-LR.json`, 156 files), queries `lr_questions` +\\r\\n  `lr_explanations` + steps + choice notes with the service key from\\r\\n  `~/ai-os/.env` (same loading as `scripts/db-pull.mjs`), applies exactly the\\r\\n  `hasAuthored` rule from `useLrExplanation.ts` (a step or a choice note with\\r\\n  prose; tags and percentages alone do not count).\\r\\n- Output `ai-os/wiki/explanations/MISSING-EXPLANATIONS.md`: a summary\\r\\n  (bank total, ingested, authored, missing, video-only), then a table per\\r\\n  PrepTest, rows `PT � S � Q � question type � reason`, where reason is\\r\\n  `not ingested` or `video only`. Sorted PT, section, question, the LSAT Lab\\r\\n  address order.\\r\\n- Run it once and commit the output to the vault (not to the app repo).\\r\\n\\r\\n### 8. Tests\\r\\n\\r\\n- `OfficialQuestionPane.test.tsx`: Explanation on \\u001a all notes expanded;\\r\\n  chevron folds one; new qid re-expands; Reset Response disabled with no\\r\\n  pick, clears the pick, leaves `answerRevealed` alone.\\r\\n- `InlineChoiceExplanation`: closed renders header but no prose.\\r\\n- `tutorBehavior.test.ts` (new): `resolveTutorLaunch` with\\r\\n  `tutorModeEnabled=false` never opens.\\r\\n- Drill.tsx wrong-submit path: existing tests that assert the tutor opens\\r\\n  after a wrong answer are updated to assert it does not and that\\r\\n  `answerLocked` stays false.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 9. Verify in the browser\\r\\n\\r\\nPreview server, a Smart Drill: submit wrong \\u001a red + green only; Explanation\\r\\n\\u001a breakdown under stimulus, five dropdowns open; fold one; Explanation again\\r\\n\\u001a all gone, colours stay; Answer again \\u001a colours gone; Reset Response \\u001a pick\\r\\ncleared, timer running; pick again + Submit \\u001a no second attempt row. Check\\r\\nthe stimulus block\'s computed padding and font before/after are identical.\\r\\nScreenshot for Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Key decisions & tradeoffs\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Flag, not deletion.** The Coach modal is 656 lines of working edge\\r\\n  function wiring. Joshua wants it back later; a constant is the cheapest\\r\\n  reversible off.\\r\\n- **Breakdown in two places.** Joshua asked for both. Same data, two\\r\\n  renderers, so no divergence risk beyond styling.\\r\\n- **Dropdowns open by default.** Reverses the documented \\"always visible,\\r\\n  no disclosure\\" choice in `InlineChoiceExplanation`. The old reasoning\\r\\n  (five clicks to read five notes) is preserved by opening expanded; the new\\r\\n  chevron only adds the ability to fold. Recorded in the component comment,\\r\\n  not an ADR: reversible in a line.\\r\\n- **Re-submit after Reset is practice.** Assumed, not confirmed by Joshua.\\r\\n  Chosen because the alternative (a second logged attempt on the same qid)\\r\\n  would double-count in history, journal and adaptive stats, and the memory\\r\\n  rule is that student work is never modified after the fact.\\r\\n- **`handleAnswerSelect` guard loosened.** Necessary for \\"nothing locks\\".\\r\\n  Sealed runs keep their gate.\\r\\n- **Explanation slot outside the stimulus element.** The whole point of the\\r\\n  spacing complaint: the stimulus `div` keeps its serif, pre-wrap and 52ch\\r\\n  measure untouched; the breakdown is a sibling with its own type.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Risks / open questions\\r\\n\\r\\n- The legacy `DrillTopBar` layout (non-official surface) also reads\\r\\n  `tutorMode`; it is not redesigned here, only kept from crashing when the\\r\\n  tutor never opens. Confirm which surfaces still render it.\\r\\n- `showSolution` / `showReviewButton` state is tangled across three submit\\r\\n  paths; the audit in step 2 may find a fourth.\\r\\n- Line-height/size of breakdown labels vs LSAT Lab is a taste call; verify\\r\\n  on screen with Joshua.\\r\\n- The census needs the service key from `~/ai-os/.env`; if it is absent the\\r\\n  script must say so and stop, not emit an empty log.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Out of scope\\r\\n\\r\\n- Any change to `TutorChatModal`, the edge function, or token budgeting.\\r\\n- Sealed runs, practice-test sittings, and the section navigator\'s opt-in\\r\\n  reveal: untouched.\\r\\n- Reading Comprehension (`RcChoiceDisclosure`) and the Classroom\\r\\n  `LRQuestionModal`, which have their own explanation layers.\\r\\n- Video explanations. 14 questions have a source video; we do not host them\\r\\n  and render no \\"Video\\" toggle.\\r\\n- Authoring the missing explanations. The log lists them; writing them is a\\r\\n  separate job.\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Warning: truncated output (original token count: 11864)\nTotal output lines: 1\n\n{"chunk_id":"155bd9","wall_time_seconds":1.1181276,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":11233,"output":"# Guided Drills & Explanations\\r\\n\\r\\nThe practice domain: how LSAT U presents drills, the questions inside them, and the teaching that wraps each one. Covers the drill surface (`src/components/drill/`, `src/pages/Drill.tsx`), the question bank (`src/lib/questionLoader.ts`, `public/data/PT*-LR.json`), and the guided-drill catalog (`src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`, `src/data/guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Language\\r\\n\\r\\n**Question Explanation**:\\r\\nThe question-level teaching shown *after* a student answers one specific question. In the schema this is the `breakdown` (conclusion, conclusionSimple, evidence, justification, objection, prediction, crucialInsight) plus `answerChoiceExplanations` (per-choice verdict + whyCorrect/whyIncorrect) on an `LRQuestion`. One per question. Currently authored for **0 of 3,964** questions - this is the headline gap (Phase 2).\\r\\n_Avoid_: explanation (ambiguous), breakdown (that\'s only one half), answer key, rationale.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Drill Instruction Card**:\\r\\nThe drill-level teaching panel shown *before* a student starts a guided drill - teaches the pattern the drill targets (\\"these six questions all hide the conclusion in the first sentence...\\"). One per guided drill. Stored as `instruction_card: { left_md, right_md }` on a guided drill. The LSAT Lab equivalent is its \\"Info\\" card (captured verbatim in `[[LSAT-LAB-LR-INFO-CARDS]]` in the vault).\\r\\n_Avoid_: info card (that\'s LSAT Lab\'s name), intro, launch card, explanation.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Question Type**:\\r\\nThe LSAT task a question asks - *Main Conclusion, Flaw, Necessary Assumption, Strengthen, Paradox, Must Be True*, etc. Stored as `questionType` on a question (and `question_types[]` on a guided drill). The bank\'s raw values are messy (110 variants, casing dupes, 67 \\"Unknown\\") and need a canonical set (~25 types).\\r\\n_Avoid_: qtype in prose (fine in code), category (that\'s Patterns/Process), question kind.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Question Feature**:\\r\\nA finer-grained pattern tag layered on top of the type - *First Sentence Conclusion, Explain Curious Fact, Anti-Causal, Famous Flaws, Conditional Diagramming, Missing Link / Idea*. Stored as `questionFeatures[]`. 60 clean values; this vocabulary effectively defines the guided-drill taxonomy.\\r\\n_Avoid_: tag, pattern (overloaded), reasoning type (that\'s a separate field).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Reasoning Type**:\\r\\nThe underlying logical structure - *Causation, Conditional Logic, Comparisons*. Stored as `reasoningType[]`. Distinct from Question Feature.\\r\\n_Avoid_: using interchangeably with Question Feature.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Drill Signature** (proposed):\\r\\nThe accurate definition of what a guided drill drills = the set of Question Types + Question Features of the *actual questions in its pool* (its qids), NOT the types/features merely mentioned in its Drill Instruction Card text.\\r\\n_Avoid_: deriving a drill\'s targets from card prose alone (it over-captures).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Guided Drill**:\\r\\nA curated, fixed practice set of LSAT questions with a Drill Instruction Card and (target state) a deterministic ordered question list. 108 exist (`guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).\\r\\n_Avoid_: drill (too generic - could mean Smart Drill), set, assignment.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer Navigator** (the \\"bubbles\\"):\\r\\nThe strip along the bottom of the official testing frame: one small empty **oval**\\r\\nper question in the queue, the question number printed *below* each oval, the\\r\\ncurrent question\'s number bold with a thin I-beam tick above it. Rendered by\\r\\n`OfficialAnswerNavigator`. Joshua calls the ovals \\"bubbles\\"; the code calls them\\r\\novals and the strip an answer navigator. Both names mean this one thing.\\r\\nOne oval stands for one **question**, never for an answer choice. This matters\\r\\nbecause \\"bubble\\" is overloaded: on paper, the bubble you fill in IS your answer\\r\\n(A-E). Here it is not. An oval is a place to go, so anything that turns it into a\\r\\nverdict on your answer is out of scope for it by definition.\\r\\n_Avoid_: pager (that is the RC per-question control that lives in the bar\'s\\r\\n`pager` slot), dots, progress bar (it is navigation, not a progress readout),\\r\\nand using \\"bubble\\" for an A-E answer choice.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Oval State**:\\r\\nWhat one oval in the Answer Navigator communicates. Today there are exactly three,\\r\\nand they are orthogonal: **current** (which question you are on), **answered** (a\\r\\nqid present in `attemptedQids`, meaning at least one recorded attempt), and\\r\\n**jumpable** (clicking moves you there, governed by `jumpEnabled`). Notably there\\r\\nis no correct/incorrect state: a section never reveals, so the strip has never had\\r\\nto say whether an answer was right.\\r\\n_Avoid_: \\"completed\\" or \\"done\\" (answered does not mean finished or correct),\\r\\n\\"selected\\" (that is an answer choice, not an oval).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Drill Launch**:\\r\\nEverything the runner needs to be told in order to start a drill: which kind of\\r\\ndrill, and the name of the particular run. It is carried in the URL and nowhere\\r\\nelse, so any drill can be refreshed, bookmarked, or reopened. A Drill Launch\\r\\nnames a run; it does not contain one. The questions, the answers already given\\r\\nand the position in the queue live in the Run Record.\\r\\n_Avoid_: route state, config (that word currently means two different things in\\r\\nthe code, a filter and a fixed question list), params, drill options.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Drill Run Spec**:\\r\\nA Drill Launch after its row has been read: the mode, the question list or the\\r\\nfilter that draws one, and the ids saying which stored row this run belongs to.\\r\\nBuilt by `drillRunSpecFromLaunch` and handed to the runner through context by\\r\\n`DrillLaunchGate`, which is the only way the runner learns what it is running.\\r\\nThe Drill Launch is what a link says; the Drill Run Spec is what the runner is\\r\\ntold. One is written and read from a URL, the other never touches one.\\r\\n_Avoid_: route state (the shape this replaced, and it read the URL a second\\r\\ntime), drill config (that word already means a filter or a fixed list), session.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Run Record**:\\r\\nThe durable row, per student per run, holding what a run actually consists of:\\r\\nthe ordered question list, every attempt, the position, and the clock already\\r\\nspent. Survives a closed tab, a sign-out, and a different device. It is the\\r\\nreason a Drill Launch can be short: the launch names the run and the Run Record\\r\\nsupplies its contents. A named generated run is keyed by that name, such as a\\r\\nBootcamp day and part or a Guided drill id, so a fresh draw cannot strand the\\r\\nrecord behind a different question list.\\r\\n_Avoid_: resume state (that is what it is for, not what it is), snapshot (that is\\r\\none write to it), session (overloaded: `DrillSession` is the in-memory object for\\r\\nthe current screen, and an auth session is a third thing).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Answer** (the pill):\\r\\nThe on/off control in the session strip that shows which choice is correct.\\r\\nOn: the correct choice\'s badge goes green, and if the student already submitted\\r\\na different choice, that one goes red. Off: both colours leave and the choices\\r\\nread as untouched. It says nothing about *why*; that is the Explanation pill.\\r\\nNeither direction locks anything and neither direction touches the logged\\r\\nattempt. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s Answer button (\\"Show correct answer\\" / hide).\\r\\n_Avoid_: reveal (the code\'s word; fine in code, but \\"reveal\\" sounds one-way and\\r\\nthis is a toggle), solution, show answer.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Explanation** (the pill):\\r\\nThe on/off control beside Answer that opens the whole Question Explanation:\\r\\nthe Argument Breakdown under the stimulus and in the drawer, and every Choice\\r\\nNote expanded. Off folds all of it away and leaves the question exactly as it\\r\\nwas, including any Answer colouring already on. Never opens on its own: a wrong\\r\\nsubmit does not press it, only the student does. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s\\r\\nExplanation button (\\"Hide explanations\\").\\r\\n_Avoid_: breakdown (that is one half of what it opens), tutor, coach.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Argument Breakdown**:\\r\\nThe question-level half of a Question Explanation: the ordered labelled steps\\r\\n(Background, Evidence, Conclusion, Evaluate, Goal, and so on) plus the style\\r\\npills (Standard / Brief / Another Way) when the source published more than one\\r\\nstyle. Lives under the stimulus while Explanation is on, and is repeated in the\\r\\ndrawer\'s Explanation tab.\\r\\n_Avoid_: explanation (the pill), breakdown steps, analysis.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Choice Note**:\\r\\nThe per-choice half of a Question Explanation: one choice\'s tag (\\"Out of\\r\\nScope\\", \\"Premise\\"), its \\"% of students\\", and its prose. Rendered as a dropdown\\r\\nunder that choice, open by default while Explanation is on, with a chevron the\\r\\nstudent can fold and reopen per choice. A choice with nothing published gets\\r\\nno dropdown at all, never an empty one.\\r\\n_Avoid_: inline explanation (the component\'s old name), answer explanation,\\r\\nrationale.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Reset Response**:\\r\\nThe control under the choices that clears the student\'s pick and nothing else.\\r\\nThe timer keeps running, the Answer and Explanation pills keep their state, and\\r\\nan attempt already logged stays logged. Greyed out until a choice is picked.\\r\\nA pick made after a Reset on an already-attempted question is practice, not a\\r\\nsecond attempt. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s Reset Response button.\\r\\n_Avoid_: undo, clear, retry (retry implies a new scored attempt).\\r\\n\\r\\n**AI Tutor**:\\r\\nThe generated coaching surfaces on the drill screen: the Coach chat that used to\\r\\nopen after a wrong answer, the \\"Ask Joshua about this question\\" hand-off on a\\r\\nquestion with no authored prose, and the \\"Explain your reasoning\\" voice chip.\\r\\nDistinct from the Question Explanation, which is transcribed LSAT Lab content\\r\\nand never generated. Off for every student as of 2026-09-12 behind one switch;\\r\\nthe code stays so it can be turned back on.\\r\\n_Avoid_: tutor mode (the code\'s flag name), Joshua (the chat\'s persona; a\\r\\nstudent reading \\"Joshua is having trouble\\" thinks the person is), coach (one of\\r\\nits tabs).\\r\\nimport * as React from \\"react\\";\\r\\nimport { DrillSplit } from \'@/components/drill/DrillSplit\';\\r\\nimport { Check, Eye, EyeOff, Flag, X } from \\"lucide-react\\";\\r\\nimport { HighlightedText } from \\"@/components/drill/HighlightedText\\";\\r\\nimport InlineChoiceExplanation, {\\r\\n  type InlineChoiceNotes,\\r\\n} from \\"@/components/drill/official/InlineChoiceExplanation\\";\\r\\nimport type { Highlight } from \\"@/lib/highlightUtils\\";\\r\\nimport { cn } from \\"@/lib/utils\\";\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface Question {\\r\\n  qid: string;\\r\\n  qnum?: number | string;\\r\\n  stimulus: string;\\r\\n  questionStem: string;\\r\\n  answerChoices: Record<string, string>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface Props {\\r\\n  question: Question;\\r\\n  displayNumber: number;\\r\\n  selectedAnswer: string;\\r\\n  eliminatedAnswers: Set<string>;\\r\\n  isFlagged: boolean;\\r\\n  answerRevealed?: boolean;\\r\\n  correctAnswer?: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Per-choice explanation content, rendered inline beneath each choice once\\r\\n   * the answer is revealed. Without it, five explanations down a page\\r\\n            // read as one undifferentiated column of text.\\r\\n            \\"mt-2 border-l-2 pl-3\\",\\r\\n            isCorrect ? \\"border-green-600/50\\" : \\"border-slate-300\\",\\r\\n          )}\\r\\n        >\\r\\n          <p\\r\\n            className={cn(\\r\\n              \\"text-[12px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide\\",\\r\\n              isCorrect ? \\"text-green-800\\" : \\"text-slate-500\\",\\r\\n            )}\\r\\n          >\\r\\n            {isCorrect ? \\"Why this is correct\\" : \\"Why this is wrong\\"}\\r\\n          </p>\\r\\n          {/* Reading size, not caption size: this is the sentence the student\\r\\n              is meant to actually read, and it carries the teaching. */}\\r\\n          <p className=\\"mt-1 whitespace-pre-line text-[15px] leading-7 text-slate-800\\">\\r\\n            {note}\\r\\n          </p>\\r\\n        </div>\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport * as React from \\"react\\";\\r\\nimport { cn } from \\"@/lib/utils\\";\\r\\nimport type { LrExplanationData, LrStyleKey } from \\"@/hooks/useLrExplanation\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The drawer\'s Explanation tab: everything about the question as a whole.\\r\\n * Per-choice content lives inline under the choices themselves\\r\\n * (`InlineChoiceExplanation`), not here.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Two rules survive from the retired `ExplanationPanel`:\\r\\n *  - The style selector renders only when the source published more than one\\r\\n *    style for this question (~5% of the bank).\\r\\n *  - Where a non-standard style carries no steps, the source said \\"(same as\\r\\n *    Standard)\\" and did not repeat the text. [];\\r\\n  const current = styles.find((s) => s.style === activeStyle) ?? styles[0];\\r\\n\\r\\n  const standardSteps = styles.find((s) => s.style === \\"standard\\")?.steps ?? [];\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (!revealed) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <Body>\\r\\n        <p className=\\"text-[13px] text-slate-400\\">\\r\\n          The explanation unlocks once you have answered or revealed this question.\\r\\n        </p>\\r\\n      </Body>\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (isLoading) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <Body>\\r\\n        <p className=\\"text-[13px] text-slate-400\\">Loading explanation.</p>\\r\\n      </Body>\\r\\n    );\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (isError) {\\r\\n    return (\\r\\n      <Body>\\r\\n        <p className=\\"text-[13px] text-slate-400\\">\\r\\n          Could not load the explanation. \\"bg-slate-900 text-white\\"\\r\\n                    : \\"border border-slate-300 bg-white text-slate-600 hover:bg-slate-100\\",\\r\\n                )}\\r\\n              >\\r\\n                {s.label}\\r\\n              </button>\\r\\n            );\\r\\n          })}\\r\\n        </div>\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n\\r\\n      {steps.length > 0 && (\\r\\n        <div className=\\"flex flex-col gap-2.5 border-t border-slate-100 pt-4\\">\\r\\n          <h3 className=\\"text-[11px] uppercase tracking-wide text-slate-400\\">\\r\\n            Argument breakdown\\r\\n            {borrowsStandard && (\\r\\n              <span className=\\"ml-2 normal-case tracking-normal text-slate-400\\">\\r\\n                same as Standard\\r\\n              </span>\\r\\n            )}\\r\\n          </h3>\\r\\n          {steps.map((step) => (\\r\\n            <div key={step.position} className=\\"text-[13px] leading-relaxed\\">\\r\\n              <span className=\\"font-semibold text-slate-900\\">{step.label}: </span>\\r\\n              <span className=\\"whitespace-pre-line text-slate-600\\">{step.body}</span>\\r\\n            </div>\\r\\n          ))}\\r\\n        </div>\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n\\r\\n      <p className=\\"border-t border-slate-100 pt-4 text-[12px] italic leading-relaxed text-slate-400\\">\\r\\n        Why each choice is right or wrong sits under that choice, beside the question.\\r\\n      </p>\\r\\n    </Body>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Body({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div className=\\"flex flex-1 flex-col gap-4 overflow-y-auto border-t border-slate-100 px-5 py-4\\">\\r\\n      {children}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n// \\"preview\\" is the pre-submission structural breakdown (Conclusion / Evidence /\\r\\n// Evaluate / Goal), answer-blind. The other three are the live coaching modes.\\r\\nexport type TutorMode = \\"preview\\" | \\"pre-answer\\" | \\"socratic\\" | \\"post-answer\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function resolveTutorMode({\\r\\n  userAnswer,\\r\\n  answerRevealed,\\r\\n}: {\\r\\n  userAnswer: string | null | undefined;\\r\\n  answerRevealed: boolean;\\r\\n}): TutorMode {\\r\\n  if (answerRevealed) return \\"post-answer\\";\\r\\n  // Before an answer is committed, the Explanation button opens the structural\\r\\n  // preview rather than streaming coaching.\\r\\n  return userAnswer ? \\"socratic\\" : \\"preview\\";\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function resolveTutorLaunch({\\r\\n  tutorModeEnabled,\\r\\n  correct,\\r\\n  answerRevealed,\\r\\n}: {\\r\\n  tutorModeEnabled: boolean;\\r\\n  correct: boolean;\\r\\n  answerRevealed: boolean;\\r\\n}): { shouldOpen: boolean; mode: TutorMode } {\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    shouldOpen: tutorModeEnabled && !correct,\\r\\n    mode: resolveTutorMode({ userAnswer: \\"submitted\\", answerRevealed }),\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\nimport { useQuery } from \'@tanstack/react-query\';\\r\\nimport { supabase } from \'@/integrations/supabase/client\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Authored LSAT Lab explanations, read from the `lr_*` tables.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * These are transcribed verbatim from the source and verified against LSAT\\r\\n * Lab\'s own API (see `ai-os/wiki/explanations/COVERAGE-AUDIT.md`). Nothing here\\r\\n * is generated: if a field is null the source did not provide it, and the UI\\r\\n * must render that absence honestly rather than filling it in.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Coverage is deliberately partial. Roughly 350 of the ~3,770 ingested\\r\\n * questions have no authored prose at all - LSAT Lab published only a video\\r\\n * for them. `hasAuthored` is the flag callers should branch on; that is the\\r\\n * signal for falling back to the AI tutor.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type LrStyleKey = \'single\' | \'standard\' | \'brief\' | \'another_way\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const LR_STYLE_LABELS: Record<LrStyleKey, string> = {\\r\\n  single: \'Explanation\',\\r\\n  standard: \'Standard\',\\r\\n  brief: \'Brief\',\\r\\n  another_way: \'Another Way\',\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Display order for the style selector; mirrors LSAT Lab\'s own ordering. */\\r\\nconst STYLE_ORDER: LrStyleKey[] = [\'standard\', \'brief\', \'another_way\', \'single\'];\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface LrChoice {\\r\\n  letter: string;\\r\\n  text: string;\\r\\n  /** Wrong-answer type, e.g. \\"Out of Scope\\". Null when the source had none. */\\r\\n  tag: string | null;\\r\\n  /** Percent of students who picked this choice. Null when not published. */\\r\\n  studentPercent: number | null;\\r\\n  isCorrect: boolean;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface LrStep {\\r\\n  position: number;\\r\\n  label: string;\\r\\n  body: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface LrStyle {\\r\\n  style: LrStyleKey;\\r\\n  label: string;\\r\\n  /** Ordered argument-breakdown steps. Empty when the source had none. */\\r\\n  steps: LrStep[];\\r\\n  /** Per-choice prose, keyed by letter. A letter may legitimately be absent. */\\r\\n  notesByLetter: Record<string, string>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface LrExplanationData {\\r\\n  questionId: string;\\r\\n  preptest: number;\\r\\n  section: number;\\r\\n  questionNumber: number;\\r\\n  questionType: string;\\r\\n  correctChoice: string | null;\\r\\n  choices: LrChoice[];\\r\\n  styles: LrStyle[];\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * True when at least one style carries real prose - a breakdown step or a\\r\\n   * per-choice note. Tags and percentages alone do not count: a question can\\r\\n   * have every choice tagged and still have no authored explanation.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  hasAuthored: boolean;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface LrExplanationQuery {\\r\\n  pt?: number | null;\\r\\n  section?: number | null;\\r\\n  questionNumber?: number | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction toStyleKey(value: string): LrStyleKey {\\r\\n  return (STYLE_ORDER as string[]).includes(value) ? (value as LrStyleKey) : \'single\';\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport async function fetchLrExplanation({\\r\\n  pt,\\r\\n  section,\\r\\n  questionNumber,\\r\\n}: LrExplanationQuery): Promise<LrExplanationData | null> {\\r\\n  if (pt == null || section == null || questionNumber == null) return null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const { data: question, error: qErr } = await supabase\\r\\n    .from(\'lr_questions\')\\r\\n    .select(\'id, preptest, section, question_number, question_type, correct_choice\')\\r\\n    .eq(\'preptest\', pt)\\r\\n    .eq(\'section\', section)\\r\\n    .eq(\'question_number\', questionNumber)\\r\\n    .maybeSingle();\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (qErr) throw qErr;\\r\\n  if (!question) return null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const [choicesRes, explRes] = await Promise.all([\\r\\n    supabase\\r\\n      .from(\'lr_answer_choices\')\\r\\n      .select(\'letter, choice_text, tag, student_percent, is_correct\')\\r\\n      .eq(\'question_id\', question.id)\\r\\n      .order(\'letter\'),\\r\\n    supabase\\r\\n      .from(\'lr_explanations\')\\r\\n      .select(\'id, style\')\\r\\n      .eq(\'question_id\', question.id),\\r\\n  ]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (choicesRes.error) throw choicesRes.error;\\r\\n  if (explRes.error) throw explRes.error;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const explanationRows = explRes.data ?? [];\\r\\n  const explanationIds = explanationRows.map((row) => row.id);\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Steps and notes are fetched for every style at once, then grouped, so the\\r\\n  // style selector switches without another round trip.\\r\\n  const [stepsRes, notesRes] = explanationIds.length\\r\\n    ? await Promise.all([\\r\\n        supabase\\r\\n          .from(\'lr_explanation_steps\')\\r\\n          .select(\'explanation_id, position, label, body\')\\r\\n          .in(\'explanation_id\', explanationIds)\\r\\n          .order(\'position\'),\\r\\n        supabase\\r\\n          .from(\'lr_explanation_choice_notes\')\\r\\n          .select(\'explanation_id, letter, body\')\\r\\n          .in(\'explanation_id\', explanationIds),\\r\\n      ])\\r\\n    : [{ data: [], error: null }, { data: [], error: null }];\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (stepsRes.error) throw stepsRes.error;\\r\\n  if (notesRes.error) throw notesRes.error;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const styles: LrStyle[] = explanationRows\\r\\n    .map((row) => {\\r\\n      const key = toStyleKey(row.style);\\r\\n      const steps = (stepsRes.data ?? [])\\r\\n        .filter((s) => s.explanation_id === row.id)\\r\\n        .map((s) => ({ position: s.position, label: s.label, body: s.body }))\\r\\n        .sort((a, b) => a.position - b.position);\\r\\n      const notesByLetter: Record<string, string> = {};\\r\\n      for (const note of notesRes.data ?? []) {\\r\\n        if (note.explanation_id === row.id) notesByLetter[note.letter] = note.body;\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      return { style: key, label: LR_STYLE_LABELS[key], steps, notesByLetter };\\r\\n    })\\r\\n    .sort((a, b) => STYLE_ORDER.indexOf(a.style) - STYLE_ORDER.indexOf(b.style));\\r\\n\\r\\n  const choices: LrChoice[] = (choicesRes.data ?? []).map((c) => ({\\r\\n    letter: c.letter,\\r\\n    text: c.choice_text,\\r\\n    tag: c.tag,\\r\\n    studentPercent: c.student_percent,\\r\\n    isCorrect: c.is_correct,\\r\\n  }));\\r\\n\\r\\n  const hasAuthored = styles.some(\\r\\n    (s) => s.steps.length > 0 || Object.keys(s.notesByLetter).length > 0,\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    questionId: question.id,\\r\\n    preptest: question.preptest,\\r\\n    section: question.section,\\r\\n    questionNumber: question.question_number,\\r\\n    questionType: question.question_type,\\r\\n    correctChoice: question.correct_choice,\\r\\n    choices,\\r\\n    styles,\\r\\n    hasAuthored,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function useLrExplanation(query: LrExplanationQuery, enabled = true) {\\r\\n  const { pt, section, questionNumber } = query;\\r\\n  return useQuery({\\r\\n    queryKey: [\'lr-explanation\', pt, section, questionNumber],\\r\\n    queryFn: () => fetchLrExplanation(query),\\r\\n    enabled: enabled && pt != null && section != null && questionNumber != null,\\r\\n    staleTime: Infinity, // authored content is immutable between ingests\\r\\n  });\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n    Directory: Users\\\\Joshu\\\\dev\\\\ai-os\\\\projects\\\\lsatu\\\\src\\\\pages\\\\Drill.tsx:4702:                  \\r\\n# 0016 - A drill link names a run, it does not contain one\\r\\n\\r\\nDate: 2026-09-01\\r\\nStatus: Accepted\\r\\n\\r\\n## Context\\r\\n\\r\\nSeventeen places start a drill. Nine build a URL; eight hand the drill to\\r\\n`/drill` in `location.state` and put nothing in the address bar. Refresh one of\\r\\nthose eight and the drill is gone: a saved set, a Smart Drill, a Testing Centre\\r\\nsection. `ptRun` is worse than gone - it never went through the resolver at all\\r\\n(`Drill.tsx:202`), so a refreshed practice-test leg loses the run id it needs to\\r\\nwrite its close-out, and that section can never be marked finished.\\r\\n\\r\\n`getResolvedDrillRouteState` reads both transports into one shape but only one\\r\\nway. Nothing turns a launch back into a URL, so the URL grammar is whatever each\\r\\ncaller happened to build, and the two can disagree: several links carry an\\r\\nassignment id *and* a copy of that assignment\'s question list.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe obvious fix is to put the questions in the URL. That is the wrong fix. There\\r\\nis already a durable per-student Run Record holding the ordered question list,\\r\\nevery attempt, the position and the clock spent, and on resume the runner adopts\\r\\nthat stored queue whole rather than re-shuffling (`Drill.tsx:786`). Refresh\\r\\nalready returns the identical run. The only thing refresh destroys is the app\'s\\r\\nability to work out *which run the student was in*.\\r\\n\\r\\nSeparately, `mode` is not the kind. Six materially different things all launch as\\r\\n`mode=type-drill`, told apart only by which extra flag rides along\\r\\n(`guidedAssignmentId`, `assignmentId`, `guidedSetId`, `practiceSlug`, `present`).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Decision\\r\\n\\r\\nA **Drill Launch** is carried in the URL and nowhere else, and it carries the\\r\\n*name* of a run, not its contents. `location.state` stops being a transport.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe launch is one discriminated union over nine kinds: Smart Drill, Section,\\r\\nSaved set, Assignment, Guided drill, Practice set, Bootcamp day, Questions (an\\r\\nad-hoc list), and Filter. A practice-test leg is a **Section with a run\\r\\nattached**, not its own kind: it runs, looks and closes out identically, and the\\r\\nonly difference is that something is waiting on the result.\\r\\n\\r\\nAnything that has an id is named by that id alone. `?set=abc123` and not the six\\r\\nqids inside it, so editing a set cannot leave an old link running last week\'s\\r\\nquestions. Only the ad-hoc list and the filter put a payload in the link, because\\r\\nthey have no row to point at. Guided drills and assignments already resolve their\\r\\nown questions (`guidedDrills.ts:82`), so nothing is lost.\\r\\n\\r\\nSwitches are split rather than pooled: the cross-cutting ones (back link,\\r\\nheading, which question to open on) sit alongside the kind, and the rest live\\r\\ninside the kind that owns them (sealed and timing on a Section, duration on a\\r\\nGuided drill). A switch that does not belong to its kind is **rejected** when it\\r\\nwould change what the student experiences, and ignored when it is decoration. A\\r\\nsealed section that silently is not sealed is a broken measurement; a missing\\r\\nheading is not.\\r\\n\\r\\n`launchDrill()` and `parseDrillLaunch()` are a matched pair and round-trip.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Consequences\\r\\n\\r\\n- A dead link (deleted assignment, deleted set, another student\'s set) shows an\\r\\n  error with a way back, rather than falling back to a generic drill. A student\\r\\n  quietly drilling something nobody assigned is worse than a student reporting a\\r\\n  broken link.\\r\\n- An id-only link costs one row read before the drill can start, and it is the\\r\\n  read that makes the link correct.\\r\\n- The drill page is not rewritten. A small converter hands it the shape it\\r\\n  already expects, so the fix lands as a diff that can be read in one sitting.\\r\\n  The converter is temporary and says so.\\r\\n- `/drill` canonicalizes its own URL on arrival, which covers any launch path\\r\\n  missed here. Two buttons still change, because they discard identity the page\\r\\n  cannot recover: saved sets throw away the set id (`SavedSets.tsx:39`), and the\\r\\n  bootcamp day button hands over picked questions without saying it was Day 3\\r\\n  homework (`TodayView.tsx:112`).\\r\\n- Today\'s URL grammar is kept as an explicitly-marked legacy branch of the\\r\\n  parser, and never written. Assigned sections go out as links that sit in a\\r\\n  student\'s inbox for weeks; breaking one is a support message from a student.\\r\\n  The branch is deletable once those links have aged out.\\r\\n- Rejected: putting the resolved question list in every URL. It duplicates the\\r\\n  Run Record, produces links too long to send, and would have made a filter\\r\\n  drill\'s identity the questions it happened to draw rather than what the\\r\\n  student asked for.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When a kind can be named by its id\\r\\n\\r\\nFound while building this, and it is the constraint that decides the remaining\\r\\nwork. Naming a run by an id is safe only when one of these holds:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Re-resolving the id yields the same questions.** A saved set and a\\r\\n   classroom assignment both store an explicit list, so re-reading the row on\\r\\n   refresh returns what the student is part-way through.\\r\\n2. **The Run Record is keyed by that id rather than by the question list.** A\\r\\n   guided drill launched from an assignment is keyed `guided::<assignmentId>`,\\r\\n   so even a different draw finds the right row and adopts its stored queue.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhere neither holds, an id-named link is actively worse than a long one: the\\r\\nrunner re-resolves, draws a different list, computes a different Run Record key\\r\\nfrom that list, finds no row, and the student loses a drill they were half way\\r\\nthrough. That rules out, for now:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **A bootcamp day.** Its questions come through a per-student recently-seen\\r\\n  filter, and its Run Record is keyed by the resulting list.\\r\\n- **A guided drill with no assignment.** It resolves through a per-student level\\r\\n  band, and without an assignment id it falls back to the same list-keyed Run\\r\\n  Record.\\r\\n\\r\\nBoth become safe the moment their Run Record is keyed by the name in the link -\\r\\nthe day, or the drill - which is a separate change and the natural next one.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Follow-up: generated runs are keyed by their name\\r\\n\\r\\nImplemented 2026-09-03. A Bootcamp day now writes its Run Record under the day\\r\\nand part, and an assignment-less Guided drill writes under its drill id. Reads\\r\\ntry that identity key first and consult the old question-list or guided-drill\\r\\nslug key only when the identity-keyed row does not exist. A completed identity\\r\\nrow therefore cannot resurrect an older in-progress run. The next snapshot\\r\\nwrites only the identity key, so an old active run migrates forward without a\\r\\nbulk data rewrite.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Follow-up: the runner reads the launch\\r\\n\\r\\nImplemented 2026-09-03. The temporary converter is gone, and with it the second\\r\\ngrammar. `drillRouteState` was a parallel reader of the same query string, and\\r\\nit agreed with the codec only by inspection. The disagreements were latent\\r\\nrather than live: `?mode=full-section&timer=70` kept its accommodated clock,\\r\\nbecause the reader that read `timer` was the one the runner used and the reader\\r\\nthat dropped it was only ever asked about canonical links. Nothing was broken.\\r\\nThe point is that nothing would have told us when that stopped being true - and\\r\\nswitching the runner onto the other reader is exactly the change that would have\\r\\nmade it true. Every such disagreement was closed before the switch, which is\\r\\nwhat the golden table of older links in `drillLaunch.test.ts` now holds in\\r\\nplace.\\r\\n\\r\\n`DrillLaunchGate` now resolves the launch and hands `Drill.tsx` a **Drill Run\\r\\nSpec** through context. The runner no longer reads the query string for anything\\r\\nabout the run: not the mode, not the assignment id, not the bootcamp day. There\\r\\nis one reader (`parseDrillLaunch`) and one shape (`drillRunSpecFromLaunch`).\\r\\n\\r\\nThe visible change is that a short link stays short. Previously the gate\\r\\nresolved `?drill=saved-set&set=.` and immediately replaced the address bar with\\r\\nthe expanded question list, which threw away the naming the whole ADR is about:\\r\\nthe first refresh was already replaying a frozen list. Now the link the student\\r\\nwas sent is the link they keep, and each refresh re-reads the row.\\r\\n\\r\\nOlder links are read but never rewritten, so everything one says has to be read\\r\\nin `parseDrillLaunch` - there is no second reader left to pick up what it drops.\\r\\nThat is why the legacy branch grew: the timer mode, the practice-test leg, the\\r\\ninitial index, and the identity ids the runner used to read for itself.\\r\\n\\r\\nAn in-memory handover is still written into the address bar, now in the\\r\\ncanonical grammar. What such a handover carries beyond a bare question list -\\r\\nthe mode, whether it is guided, whether it is sealed - travels as a `RunCarry`\\r\\nunder its own params, so writing it out and reading it back cannot quietly turn\\r\\na `natural-drill` into a type-drill.\\r\\n# 0011 - An oval is a question, not a verdict\\r\\n\\r\\nDate: 2026-08-31\\r\\nStatus: Accepted\\r\\n\\r\\n## Context\\r\\n\\r\\nA student who builds their own LR set could only move forward through it. A full\\r\\nsection has always had the LawHub bottom strip - one oval per question, click any\\r\\nof them to jump - and a built set had nothing equivalent, so the questions the\\r\\nstudent chose could only be walked in the order they came out.\\r\\n\\r\\nTwo things were actually wrong, and only one of them was missing work:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. `Drill.tsx` had been passing `onPrev` and `onAdvance` to `OfficialSmartDrillBar`\\r\\n   for some time, but the bar never rendered those pills. The handlers were\\r\\n   received and dropped. This was a bug, not a gap.\\r\\n2. The oval strip genuinely did not exist for a built set.\\r\\n\\r\\nFixing (2) raised a question a section never had to answer. A section never\\r\\nreveals, so its ovals could only ever mean \\"answered\\". A built set *does* reveal:\\r\\nthe moment a question is revealed the app knows whether the student got it right,\\r\\nand that information could be shown on the oval - green for correct, red for\\r\\nmissed, jump straight to your misses.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Decision\\r\\n\\r\\nThe strip stays exactly as rich as the section\'s. An oval carries three states and\\r\\nno more: **current**, **answered**, **jumpable**. It never shows whether the answer\\r\\nwas right, in a set or anywhere else.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe reason is what an oval *is*. One oval stands for one **question** - a place to\\r\\ngo. It does not stand for an answer choice, even though on paper the bubble you\\r\\nfill in is precisely your answer. Colouring ovals by correctness quietly recasts a\\r\\nnavigation control as a scorecard, and then the strip is doing two jobs and reads\\r\\nas neither.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe strip is extracted into `AnswerOvals` so the section and the built set render\\r\\none component rather than two copies that drift apart.\\r\\n\\r\\nJumping commits any pending selection before it moves, for the same reason\\r\\nPrevious does: leaving a question must never be the thing that loses the answer on\\r\\nit.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Consequences\\r\\n\\r\\n- A built set now navigates like a section: ovals, Previous, Next, free movement.\\r\\n- Wanting a right/wrong overview after a set is a real want, and it now has an\\r\\n  obvious home that is *not* the ovals - the post-set review screen, where being a\\r\\n  scorecard is the job.\\r\\n- `AnswerOvals` is shared, so a change to oval appearance lands in both surfaces at\\r\\n  once. That is the point, and it also means neither surface can quietly diverge.\\r\\n- Rejected: colouring on reveal, and colouring only after the set ends. Both were\\r\\n  offered and both were declined on the grounds above.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

[\\r\\n3939:       {\\r\\n3940:         label: officialLeadLabel,\\r\\n3941:         onClick: () => navigate(guidedDrillReturnTo)\\r\\n3942:       },\\r\\n3943:       {\\r\\n3944:         label: [sectionLabel, questionLabel].filter(Boolean).join(\' � \')\\r\\n3945:       }\\r\\n3946:     ] : [\\r\\n3947:       // Lead with the practice test, not a generic \\"Library\\" crumb - the\\r\\n3948:       // student already knows where they are; what they want named is the PT.\\r\\n3949:       {\\r\\n3950:         label: officialLeadLabel,\\r\\n3951:         onClick: () => navigate(\'/classroom/drill\'),\\r\\n3952:       },\\r\\n3953:       {\\r\\n3954:         label: [sectionLabel, questionLabel].filter(Boolean).join(\' � \')\\r\\n3955:       },\\r\\n3956:     ];\\r\\n3957:     const handleEndBootcampPracticeDrill = async () => {\\r\\n3958:       if (isEndingBootcampPracticeDrill) return;\\r\\n3959:       setIsEndingBootcampPracticeDrill(true);\\r\\n3960:       try {\\r\\n3961:         await handleFinishSection(undefined, { returnToAfterFinish: bootcampPracticeReturnTo });\\r\\n3962:       } catch (err) {\\r\\n3963:         console.error(\'[drill:intro-to-lr-practice:end] finish failed\', err);\\r\\n3964:         setIsEndingBootcampPracticeDrill(false);\\r\\n3965:       }\\r\\n3966:     };\\r\\n3967:     const officialIsPaused = !!timer?.isPaused && !isEndingBootcampPracticeDrill;\\r\\n3968: \\r\\n3969:     return (\\r\\n3970:       <>\\r\\n3971:         <AlertDialog open={showExitDialog} onOpenChange={setShowExitDialog}>\\r\\n3972:           <AlertDialogContent>\\r\\n3973:             <AlertDialogHeader>\\r\\n3974:               <AlertDialogTitle>Leave drill session?</AlertDialogTitle>\\r\\n3975:               <AlertDialogDescription>\\r\\n3976:                 Are you sure you want to leave? Your progress in this session will be saved.\\r\\n3977:               </AlertDialogDescription>\\r\\n3978:             </AlertDialogHeader>\\r\\n3979:             <AlertDialogFooter>\\r\\n3980:               <AlertDialogCancel>Cancel</AlertDialogCancel>\\r\\n3981:               <AlertDialogAction onClick={confirmExit}>Yes, leave</AlertDialogAction>\\r\\n3982:             </AlertDialogFooter>\\r\\n3983:           </AlertDialogContent>\\r\\n3984:         </AlertDialog>\\r\\n3985: \\r\\n3986:         <AlertDialog open={submitConfirmOpen} onOpenChange={setSubmitConfirmOpen}>\\r\\n3987:           <AlertDialogContent>\\r\\n3988:             <AlertDialogHeader>\\r\\n3989:               <AlertDialogTitle>Submit this section?</AlertDialogTitle>\\r\\n3990:               <AlertDialogDescription>\\r\\n3991:                 {(() => {\\r\\n3992:                   const unanswered = session.questionQueue.filter(\\r\\n3993:                     (qid) => !session.attempts.get(qid)?.selectedAnswer,\\r\\n3994:                   ).length;\\r\\n3995:                   const timeLeft =\\r\\n3996:                     timer?.mode === \'countdown\' && timer.ms > 0 ? timer.label : null;\\r\\n3997:                   if (unanswered === 0 && !timeLeft) {\\r\\n3998:                     return \'This ends the section and scores it.\';\\r\\n3999:                   }\\r\\n4000:                   return [\\r\\n4001:                     unanswered > 0\\r\\n32: import { TutorChatModal } from \'@/components/drill/TutorChatModal\';\\r\\n1864:             setTutorChatOpen(true);\\r\\n1963:           setTutorChatOpen(true);\\r\\n2120:         setTutorChatOpen(true);\\r\\n3508:   const openTutorForCurrent = (tab?: \'coach\' | \'breakdown\' | \'notes\') => {\\r\\n3517:     setTutorChatOpen(true);\\r\\n3659:           <TutorChatModal\\r\\n3818:       onAskTutor: () => openTutorForCurrent(),\\r\\n4281:                     setTutorChatOpen(true);\\r\\n4431:             setTutorChatOpen(true);\\r\\nimport * as React from \\"react\\";\\r\\nimport { cn } from \\"@/lib/utils\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The per-choice explanation layer that sits directly beneath an answer choice\\r\\n * in `OfficialQuestionPane`. Replaces the old dark `ExplanationPanel` sheet.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The explanation is ALWAYS VISIBLE. It used to sit behind a \\"Why this is\\r\\n * wrong\\" disclosure, which cost a click per choice and, on a shared screen\\r\\n * during teaching, hid the very thing being taught. Reading five choices meant\\r\\n * five clicks. The prose is the point of the review screen, so it is set at\\r\\n * reading size and shown outright.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Two rules carried over from that panel, and tightened to apply per choice\\r\\n * rather than per question:\\r\\n *  - Never invent content. A missing tag, percentage, or note renders as\\r\\n *    absent. A choice with no note gets no disclosure row at all - not an\\r\\n *    empty one, not a placeholder.\\r\\n *  - The source\'s nuance lives in the tag chip verbatim (\\"Too Weak\\", \\"Weakens,\\r\\n *    if anything\\"). The disclosure label only says which way the choice went.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface InlineChoiceNote {\\r\\n  /** Wrong-answer type from the source, e.g. \\"Out of Scope\\". */\\r\\n  tag?: string | null;\\r\\n  /** Percent of students who picked this choice. */\\r\\n  studentPercent?: number | null;\\r\\n  /** The choice\'s explanation prose. Absent for letters the source skipped. */\\r\\n  note?: string | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Per-letter inline content for one question. A letter may legitimately be absent. */\\r\\nexport type InlineChoiceNotes = Record<string, InlineChoiceNote>;\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface Props {\\r\\n  letter: string;\\r\\n  isCorrect: boolean;\\r\\n  /** The student\'s own pick, marked so they can find it fast. */\\r\\n  isSelected: boolean;\\r\\n  content: InlineChoiceNote;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** True when there is nothing real to render for this letter. */\\r\\nexport function isInlineChoiceEmpty(content: InlineChoiceNote | undefined, isSelected: boolean) {\\r\\n  if (isSelected) return false;\\r\\n  if (!content) return true;\\r\\n  return !content.tag && content.studentPercent == null && !content.note;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function InlineChoiceExplanation({\\r\\n  letter,\\r\\n  isCorrect,\\r\\n  isSelected,\\r\\n  content,\\r\\n}: Props) {\\r\\n  const { tag, studentPercent, note } = content;\\r\\n  const hasNote = typeof note === \\"string\\" && note.trim().length > 0;\\r\\n\\r\\n  if (isInlineChoiceEmpty(content, isSelected)) return null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div className=\\"mt-2.5\\">\\r\\n      {/* Tag, percentage, and the student\'s own pick. Any of these may be absent. */}\\r\\n      <div className=\\"flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2 text-[12px]\\">\\r\\n        {tag && (\\r\\n          <span\\r\\n            className={cn(\\r\\n              \\"rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-semibold\\",\\r\\n              isCorrect ? \\"bg-green-600 text-white\\" : \\"bg-slate-200 text-slate-700\\",\\r\\n            )}\\r\\n#!/usr/bin/env node\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * db-pull.mjs - read a table out of the live database, and only read it.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Read-only by construction: it speaks PostgREST over HTTP GET, so there is no\\r\\n * statement to get wrong and no way for this to write, drop or migrate\\r\\n * anything. That is the point. Arbitrary SQL against production is available\\r\\n * through the Lovable MCP connector, but that tool cannot distinguish a SELECT\\r\\n * from a DROP, so it stays gated and deliberately awkward. Pulling rows for an\\r\\n * audit should not require opening that door.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Needs a service-role key, because RLS hides every row from the anon key and\\r\\n * an empty result is indistinguishable from an empty table. Set it in\\r\\n * ~/ai-os/.env (which is git-ignored and already holds every other credential):\\r\\n *\\r\\n *   SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=<the service_role key from the Supabase dashboard,\\r\\n *                              Project Settings -> API. It is a long JWT\\r\\n *                              starting \\"eyJ\\", not the short anon key.>\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Usage:\\r\\n *   node scripts/db-pull.mjs attempts > /tmp/attempts.json\\r\\n *   node scripts/db-pull.mjs attempts id,user_id,pt,section,qnum,selected_answer,correct\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Pairs with the grading auditor:\\r\\n *   node scripts/db-pull.mjs attempts > /tmp/attempts.json\\r\\n *   node scripts/audit-attempt-grading.mjs /tmp/attempts.json\\r\\n */\\r\\nimport fs from \'node:fs\';\\r\\nimport path from \'node:path\';\\r\\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \'node:url\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));\\r\\nconst REPO = path.resolve(__dirname, \'..\');\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction readEnv(file) {\\r\\n  const out = {};\\r\\n  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;\\r\\n  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, \'utf8\').split(\'\\\\n\')) {\\r\\n    const t = line.trim();\\r\\n    if (!t || t.startsWith(\'#\')) continue;\\r\\n    const i = t.indexOf(\'=\');\\r\\n    if (i < 0) continue;\\r\\n    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^[\\"\']|[\\"\']$/g, \'\');\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst env = {\\r\\n  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, \'../../.env\')),\\r\\n  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, \'.env\')),\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nconst table = process.argv[2];\\r\\nconst columns = process.argv[3] ?? \'*\';\\r\\nif (!table) {\\r\\n  console.error(\'usage: node scripts/db-pull.mjs <table> [columns]\');\\r\\n  process.exit(2);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst url = env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL;\\r\\nconst key = env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY;\\r\\n\\r\\nif (!url) {\\r\\n  console.error(\'db-pull: VITE_SUPABASE_URL is not set in projects/lsatu/.env\');\\r\\n  process.exit(2);\\r\\n}\\r\\nif (!key) {\\r\\n  console.error(\\r\\n    \'db-pull: SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY is not set.\\\\n\' +\\r\\n      \'  Add it to ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored). Supabase dashboard -> Project Settings -> API\\\\n\' +\\r\\n      \'  -> service_role. Without it RLS returns zero rows and an empty table and a hidden\\\\n\' +\\r\\n      \'  one look identical, so this refuses to guess rather than reporting a false empty.\',\\r\\n  );\\r\\n  process.exit(2);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n// Page through: PostgREST caps a response, and a silently truncated pull is the\\r\\n// failure mode that makes an audit say \\"all clear\\" over rows it never saw.\\r\\nconst PAGE = 1000;\\r\\nconst rows = [];\\r\\nfor (let from = 0; ; from += PAGE) {\\r\\n  const res = await fetch(`${url}/rest/v1/${table}?select=${encodeURIComponent(columns)}`, {\\r\\n    headers: {\\r\\n      apikey: [REDACTED],\\r\\n      Authorization: [REDACTED] ${key}`,\\r\\n      Range: `${from}-${from + PAGE - 1}`,\\r\\n      Prefer: \'count=exact\',\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  });\\r\\n  if (!res.ok) {\\r\\n    console.error(`db-pull: ${res.status} ${res.statusText}\\\\n${(await res.text()).slice(0, 400)}`);\\r\\n    process.exit(1);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  const batch = await res.json();\\r\\n  rows.push(...batch);\\r\\n  if (batch.length < PAGE) break;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nprocess.stderr.write(`db-pull: ${rows.length} row(s) from ${table}\\\\n`);\\r\\nprocess.stdout.write(JSON.stringify({ rows }, null, 2));\\r\\nimport * as React from \'react\';\\r\\nimport { cn } from \'@/lib/utils\';\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The two-column exam body shared by the LR and RC players.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Both players show a reading surface on the left and the question with its\\r\\n * answer choices on the right, each column scrolling on its own behind a\\r\\n * hairline divider. They drifted apart once already - the LR side lost its page\\r\\n * cap and the columns stopped lining up with the bottom bar - so the geometry\\r\\n * lives here and nowhere else. A spacing fix is made once.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * What stays configurable is the column ratio, the page cap, and whether the\\r\\n * right column is present at all (RC\'s Passage Only View, and the LR\\r\\n * pool-exhausted state, both render a single full-width pane).\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Both drill runners currently pass `passage-weighted` with no page cap, so the\\r\\n * defaults below are for other callers rather than for them. The doc used to\\r\\n * say LR split evenly and capped at max-w-7xl, which stopped being true when\\r\\n * the LR pane was rebuilt and was never corrected.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type DrillSplitRatio = \'even\' | \'passage-weighted\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst RATIO_CLASS: Record<DrillSplitRatio, string> = {\\r\\n  even: \'grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-2\',\\r\\n  \'passage-weighted\': \'grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-[minmax(0,53fr)_minmax(0,47fr)]\',\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface Props {\\r\\n  left: React.ReactNode;\\r\\n  /** Omit for a single full-width column. */\\r\\n  right?: React.ReactNode;\\r\\n  ratio?: DrillSplitRatio;\\r\\n  /** Tailwind cap for the page width, or null to fill the frame. */\\r\\n  maxWidthClassName?: string | null;\\r\\n  className?: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function DrillSplit({\\r\\n  left,\\r\\n  right,\\r\\n  ratio = \'even\',\\r\\n  maxWidthClassName = \'max-w-7xl\',\\r\\n  className,\\r\\n}: Props) {\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div\\r\\n      className={cn(\\r\\n        \'grid min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-hidden\',\\r\\n        right ? RATIO_CLASS[ratio] : \'grid-cols-1\',\\r\\n        maxWidthClassName && `w-full ${maxWidthClassName} mx-auto`,\\r\\n        className,\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    >\\r\\n      {left}\\r\\n      {right}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default DrillSplit;\\r\\n"}'}]
